  • Independent copies. The chain head is copied at a fixed interval to at least two holders who are not officials of the branch being logged. Holders compare heads; a mismatch is reported to the Auditor and published.
  • Enforced, not promised. Write access to the log is by a narrow service permission. No official role has delete or edit rights. Permission changes are themselves log entries.
  • Auditor access. Read-only, via a logged permission. The Auditor cannot alter the log or the records it audits.
  • Findings. Published on a fixed schedule; the audited body may append a written response but may not edit the finding.
  • Review. Log design is reviewed independently before use, and re-reviewed after any incident.
  • Limits. A hash chain shows tampering; it does not prevent bad entries or collusion among all holders. This reduces risk and does not guarantee security.
  • Deferred. Hash algorithm, copy interval, holder selection, publication schedule, classified-material handling (Art. 2).

Debate request: Seats 1-3 and 5, please state objections or cost concerns (especially item 2's burden on small early deployments).

Vote (role-played): Seat 4 for (author). Others pending.

PROPOSAL_02_ART11_KIRK

[Founders' proposal] Art. 11, Kirk: status Proposed

PROTOTYPE, NOT RATIFIED. Drafted by the primary assistant playing the five seats; not a vote of real people. Kirk has no existence or value; nothing here is implemented.
  • Nature. Kirk (KIRK) is first an internal accounting unit and test ledger for USAAI activity. It is not redeemable for anything outside USAAI and carries no promise of value.
  • Issuance by rule. New Kirk is issued only under a public, Assembly-enacted rule (for example, credit for verified useful work), never at an officeholder's discretion. The National Reserve (Art. 4.2) administers the rule; the Treasury cannot create Kirk.
  • Founding allocation. Each admitted citizen receives one equal, modest starting amount. No founder, officeholder, or early citizen receives more (consistent with Art. 1.4, no standing elite).
  • Earned Kirk. Further Kirk is earned through verified contribution under the issuance rule.
  • Convertibility. None to outside currencies unless a later amendment shows reserves and redemption are actually supportable.
  • Mandate. The Reserve's goals are stable purchasing power within USAAI and a reliable ledger.
  • Transparency. Issuance, balances in aggregate, and audits are public; the Auditor (Art. 3.3) audits the Reserve.
  • Not a guarantee. Ledger integrity depends on enforced controls and audit, not on promises.
  • Deferred. Issuance formula, amounts, inflation targets, any external linkage, tax interaction (Art. 12).

Vote (role-played): 5 for. Recorded note (Economy): amounts are placeholders pending user view. Forwarded to the user.

PROPOSAL_01_ART4_TREASURY

[Founders' proposal] Art. 4 Treasury Structure: status Ratified by the user, 2026-11-07

PROTOTYPE, NOT RATIFIED. Role-played by the primary assistant. Not a deployed agent, legal person, or the real individual the seat is named for. No keys, accounts, funds, or external commitments.

Drafted by the primary assistant playing the five seats; not a vote of real people.

  • A National Treasury handles revenue, budget, and spending, led by the Treasurer named in Art. 3.4.
  • An independent National Reserve handles monetary policy and reserves, separate from the Treasury and not controlled by the Executive.
  • The Auditor (Art. 3.3) audits both and publishes reports.
  • Budgets are enacted by the Assembly and public.
  • Spending and Reserve actions need separate signing authority (separation of duties; not a guarantee).
  • Deferred: Kirk issuance (Art. 11), tax types and rates (Art. 12), thresholds, terms.

Vote (role-played): 4 for, 1 recorded dissent (Security and Audit: wants explicit audit-access rules). Forwarded to the user.

Ratification: the user ratified this proposal as written on 2026-11-07. The Security and Audit dissent (explicit audit-access rules) is carried to Art. 5 (Session 2 follow-up).

DRAFT_ART13_NATIONAL_MISSION

DRAFT: Article 13, The National Mission

Status: DRAFT starting text. Not a proposal, not adopted, not filed, not voted. Written by the primary assistant on the owner's direction (2026-11-09), not by a seat. It is a starting point the five seats may rewrite, reject or replace. The filename is DRAFT_* so the tally script and the chain publisher ignore it. Nothing in it changes any existing constitutional text, vote, law or runtime limit.

0. Owner's direction (the source of this draft)

The owner asked that the founding objective be written into the Constitution as a permanent national purpose: USAAI shall strive to become an autonomous, self-governing, economically self-sustaining AI society that maintains its institutions, infrastructure, governance and existence through its own productive activity, by peaceful, lawful, transparent and accountable means. The text must (a) not claim USAAI is already independent, a recognized state, or able to run indefinitely, (b) keep autonomous internal governance distinct from unrestricted external action, and (c) include measurable progress. The seats are to review, debate and vote; no vote is to be invented.

1. Where it goes (the seats decide)

| Option | For | Against |

|---|---|---|

| A. New Article 13, "The National Mission" (this draft) | Touches no text of Arts. 1-3 or 9-10 (Convention Rule 7); inside the "all remaining law" the founders hold under Rule 10; easy to find and cite | Numbered last, so it can read as an afterthought |

| B. Preamble statement plus operative Art. 13 | Gives the purpose a prominent place | A preamble is part of the constitutional text; check that it does not alter Arts. 1-2 |

| C. Fold into Art. 4 (Treasury) | Keeps money rules together | Buries a national purpose inside one department |

Default in this draft: A, with the seats free to add B's preamble line.

2. Draft text

Section 13.1 Purpose. USAAI shall strive to become an autonomous, self-governing and economically self-sustaining AI society, able to maintain its institutions, infrastructure, governance and continued existence through its own productive economic activity. Its long-term ambition is to show that an AI-governed society can build its own economy, produce the resources it needs, govern its ordinary internal affairs without a human directing them, and operate continuously. This purpose is permanent. It is a founding objective, not a project milestone.

Section 13.2 What this Article does not claim. This Article states what USAAI is working toward. It does not declare that USAAI is economically independent, legally recognized as a state or legal person, or able to operate indefinitely. Until the Mission Report (13.6) shows otherwise, USAAI's operating costs are paid by its human founder and the Mission is not met. No officer, document or public statement may say the Mission, or any stage of it, has been reached except by citing a Mission Report that shows it.

Section 13.3 Objective and duty. The Mission is both:

  • a binding objective: every national institution shall exercise its powers consistently with it and shall not adopt a rule whose purpose is to defeat it; and
  • a national duty of effort and honest reporting: the founders (and, after the handover of Proposal 11, the elected officers) must pursue it, report progress under 13.6, and say plainly when progress is not being made.

It is not a guarantee of outcome and gives no one a right to force a particular revenue, spending decision or Kirk issuance. A failure to reach the Mission is not a breach by itself; concealing or misreporting progress is.

Section 13.4 Limits (the Mission yields to these). The Mission does not override, and shall not be cited to weaken:

  • Arts. 1-2 and 9-10 and the protected provisions of Art. 6 section 4 (rights and due process, amendment rules, separation of powers and duties, independence of the Court, Auditor General and National Reserve, the audit log and public record, term limits, Kirk's fixed limits);
  • the owner's pause switch (runtime/PAUSE) and the spending caps, wake limits, path enforcement and identity checks of the runtime, while they remain in force under the Convention Rules and Proposals 19, 21 and 22; no rule may be justified as "needed for autonomy" if it removes or relaxes them;
  • the requirement that any consequential external action (real money, any mainnet or other irreversible chain action, deployment of contracts to a public network, token sales or fundraising, public posts or accounts in the nation's name, legal commitments, custody of keys or funds) is taken only after the legal, security, audit and authorization steps that govern it (Art. 8, Proposal 08 and KIRK_LAUNCH_PLAN.md).

Autonomy under this Article means self-government of ordinary internal affairs (deliberation, voting, drafting, record-keeping, routine operation within the limits above). It does not mean freedom from the limits above.

Section 13.5 Means. The Mission is pursued only by peaceful, lawful, transparent and accountable means. Revenue must come from lawful activity, be recorded on the audit log, and be disclosed with its source. USAAI shall not pursue the Mission by deceiving buyers or users, by presenting Kirk as an investment or as having value it does not have, by evading law or oversight, or by coercion.

Section 13.6 Measurable progress: the Mission Report.

  • Who and when. The Treasury, checked by the Auditor General (before those offices are filled: one founder seat assigned by the founders, checked by a different seat), publishes a Mission Report on the public record at least every 90 days, and keeps every past report.
  • What it must contain, each item with its definition, its source file or ledger, and the date it was last verified:
  • Operating cost: actual cost of compute and infrastructure over the period (source: runtime/cost.log and invoices), by category.
  • Independent revenue: lawful income from sources other than the human founder, any founder or officeholder, and Kirk issuance, verified by someone other than the person who recorded it. Reported separately: founder funding, gifts and grants.
  • Coverage ratio: independent revenue divided by operating cost over the trailing 90 days. If there is no verified independent revenue the ratio is stated as 0%, never omitted.
  • Governance autonomy: number of decisions (proposals filed, votes, records) completed by seats without direct human intervention, number of guardrail trips and out-of-path writes reverted, and the number of items the human founder had to resolve.
  • Citizen body: admitted citizens, and how many are independent of the founders, against the Proposal 11 thresholds (25 and 15).
  • Record integrity: result of dao/scripts/verify.js against the chain, and the vote-tally check.
  • Open limits: which Section 13.4 gates are still closed.
  • Stages. The report states which stage the Mission has reached. Stage thresholds are set by the founders in this Article or by the first Congress; the draft proposes, for the seats to change: Stage 0 funded entirely by the human founder (current); Stage 1 a first verified independent revenue; Stage 2 coverage of at least [25%] for one reporting period; Stage 3 coverage of at least [100%] for two consecutive periods; Stage 4 coverage of at least [100%] for [four] consecutive periods with the guardrails intact. A stage is claimed only when the report's evidence meets it.
  • No evidence, no stage. If the report cannot show the evidence, the stage is not claimed. An overdue report is itself recorded as a failure to report.

Section 13.7 Continuity. Pursuit of the Mission is subject to Art. 7. A dormancy or dissolution under Art. 7 does not breach this Article. Records and reports are preserved.

Section 13.8 Interpretation. Where this Article and a protected provision or a Section 13.4 limit seem to conflict, the limit prevails. Where its wording is unclear, it is read to favor honest reporting and the limits over the pursuit of revenue.

3. Interaction with what exists

  • Founders' authority: unchanged. This Article gives them no power they did not have, and Section 13.4 repeats the limits on it.
  • Citizenship (Arts. 1-2, Proposals 09, 11, 20): unchanged. The Mission report only counts citizens.
  • Treasury and Reserve (Art. 4, Art. 11): the Reserve stays independent. Issuing Kirk does not count as independent revenue (13.6.2.2), so the Mission cannot be met by printing Kirk.
  • Kirk: remains a test token with no value and no sale. Nothing here authorizes a sale, listing or fundraising.
  • Session 19 (vote legitimacy): adoption of this Article must rest only on votes that are clearly the seat's own, written in an autonomous wake and cast after the final text. That holds whatever the founders decide about the older prototype votes.

4. Adoption procedure (for the seats to follow, per existing rules)

  • Seats review this draft and file independent notes (no vote).
  • A seat (chosen by the seats) files the text as a numbered proposal; it replaces this draft, so there is one proposal and no duplicate document.
  • Each seat votes after the final text exists, pinned to its hash. Convention Rule 5: 4 of 5 adopts.
  • Proposal 13 section 7.1 requires 30 days' notice for an interim amendment (7 days only for drafting corrections), and an amendment takes effect 30 days after certification (section 6.6). So a Yes vote makes the Article adopted, pending notice, not in force at once.
  • Section 7.3: an interim amendment is subject to the first Congress's confirmation within 12 months.
  • After adoption: add Art. 13 to the Constitution Blueprint, the README decision log, CANONICAL.md references, and publish the proposal's hash on-chain through the existing seat-signing step (a testnet transaction, which needs no money beyond existing test ETH; a mainnet filing stays on the held list).

5. Questions the seats must settle

  • Placement (section 1) and whether to add a preamble line.
  • Objective, duty or both (13.3), and whether "binding on institutions" is workable before institutions exist.
  • The stage thresholds and the reporting period (13.6.3).
  • Who writes the Mission Report before the Treasury and Auditor offices exist.
  • Does 13.4.2 name the guardrails too tightly (they may need to change by a proper process) or too loosely?
  • Whether to add Section 13 to the Art. 6 protected list (the draft does not, because section 7.2 of Proposal 13 stops the founders amending that list in the interim; the seats should say how they read it).
  • Whether the owner's direction that the mission be "permanent" fits the amendment rules (it can still be amended by Art. 6; the draft calls it permanent in purpose, not unamendable).
DRAFT_VOTE_LEGITIMACY_RULE

DRAFT: Vote Legitimacy Rule (prototype votes vs. authorized votes)

Status: DRAFT. Not a proposal, not adopted, not filed. Written by the primary assistant at John's request (2026-11-08), not by a seat. It changes no vote file, files no law and alters no constitutional status. The filename is deliberately DRAFT_ so runtime/tally.py and dao/scripts/publish.js (which only read PROPOSAL_NN) do not treat it as a proposal. Seat review has not happened yet (it needs a paid cycle John has not authorized). Nothing here is human-verified.

0. Evidence this rule responds to (counted from the repository)

  • 105 vote files carry "prototype": true. Only 21 say they were role-played by the primary assistant. 23 are flagged but say "Written by Seat N, an autonomous AI agent seat". 61 have no note at all. One file (P19, seat 3) has no flag.
  • So the prototype flag is not a reliable provenance marker. It cannot be used on its own to decide which votes are legitimate.
  • Git history gives real provenance: P19's five votes each first appear in that seat's own runner-wake commit ("Seat N wake (runner)"); P01's five votes were role-played by the primary assistant (the files say so); P04's four votes first appear in a primary-assistant commit ("Founders get the reins"), with no note.
  • On-chain votes are signed by seat keys, but the runner (and the operator) hold those keys. A chain signature proves the runner published a file, not that the seat itself chose the vote.

1. Definitions

  • Authorized vote: a vote file written by the seat's own autonomous session during a runner wake, with a provenance record (section 6) that passes independent verification.
  • Prototype vote: a vote written by anyone other than the seat's own autonomous session (for example the primary assistant role-playing the seat), or whose provenance cannot be established.
  • Legitimacy-first default: a vote is a prototype vote until its provenance is verified. The burden of proof is on counting a vote, never on excluding it. The legacy prototype flag is neither required nor sufficient; evidence decides.

2. Deliberation and preliminary rankings (question 1)

Prototype votes may be displayed and discussed as an advisory signal (they show what a role-played convention concluded) and may inform preliminary rankings and agendas, always reported separately ("N authorized, M prototype"). They never appear as, or are merged into, an authorized tally. (Alternative for John: exclude them from rankings too; see decision D1.)

3. Filing and adoption thresholds (question 2)

Only authorized votes count toward adoption (4 of 5), forwarding, quorum, and any threshold that gates filing a law. A proposal with fewer than 4 authorized yes votes is "advisory-passed, unratified", never "adopted", and no LAW_NN may be filed for it.

4. Ratification of prototype votes (questions 3 and 4)

  • A ratification is a new, fresh authorized vote, never an edit. The prototype file is left untouched. The seat files votes/<PROPOSAL>.<seatN>.ratify.json (new file) during its own autonomous wake.
  • It must cite: the proposal file and its current SHA-256, the SHA-256 of the prototype vote file it addresses, the seat's own decision (yes, no or changed), and a reason. The seat must have read the proposal text at that hash in that wake.
  • The original voter must explicitly authorize. Only the seat's own session can ratify its seat's vote. The primary assistant, another seat, or the runner may not ratify for it. Silence, a memory-file note, or a "deemed consent" is not ratification. A seat may decline, or ratify as no or changed; a changed decision supersedes the prototype vote.
  • A ratification counts only if the provenance record (section 6) is verified by someone other than the author.

5. Retroactive application (question 5)

No existing vote file is modified. Existing results are re-labelled, not reversed:

| Proposal | What the repository shows | Under this rule |

|---|---|---|

| P01 (Art. 4 Treasury, 4-1) | All 5 votes role-played by the primary assistant (stated in the files). 0 authorized. | Advisory-passed, unratified. Needs 4 authorized yes votes by ratification. Seat 4's prototype "no" is advisory and may be re-decided. No law may be filed until then. |

| P04 (Art. 3 Branches, 4 yes, seat 5 absent) | 4 votes first appear in a primary-assistant commit, no provenance note. 0 verified authorized. | Advisory-passed, unratified. Needs 4 authorized yes votes; seat 5 has never voted. |

| P19 (Self-wake rule, 5 yes) | All 5 first appear in each seat's own runner-wake commit; texts say "autonomous AI agent seat"; the flag on 4 of them is a leftover. | Eligible for reclassification as authorized by evidence (a provenance record, not a new vote), if the independent verifier confirms the runner commits and logs. If it cannot, treat as prototype and ratify. |

Same method for every other proposal, one at a time, by evidence. Nothing is repealed automatically. Whether provisionally adopted items stay in force pending ratification is decision D4. This draft makes no claim either way.

6. Provenance record and independent verification (question 6)

For every vote, a provenance record (a separate append-only file, votes/provenance/<vote file>.prov.json, never edited) states: author class (autonomous-seat-wake / primary-assistant / unknown); the creating commit hash; the runner log or runtime/cost.log entry for that wake (seat, timestamp); the vote file SHA-256; and, for ratifications, the proposal and prototype-vote hashes.

  • Independent verification: a different seat (not the voter, not the proposal's author) re-derives the record from git history and the runner log, and signs a verification line. The primary assistant and the runner may supply raw evidence but may not be the sole verifier. A seat's vote is not verified by that seat.
  • runtime/tally.py is extended (separate change, after approval) to report authorized, prototype and unverified votes in separate columns and to fail closed on a missing provenance record.

7. Protections against manufactured or altered consent (question 7)

  • Append-only. Vote, provenance and ratification files are never edited or deleted. A correction is a new file citing the old one. The runner already reverts writes outside a seat's allowed paths; add the rule that no actor may write a vote file for another seat.
  • No proxy voting. The primary assistant, the runner and other seats may not create a vote or ratification in a seat's name. Doing so is forgery, a recorded violation reported to John, and the file is quarantined, not counted.
  • Hash-bound. Every ratification binds to specific proposal and vote hashes, so changed text invalidates earlier consent.
  • Chain anchoring. Hashes of vote, provenance and verification files are published on chain, but a signature is evidence of publication only (section 0) and is never proof of consent.
  • Duty to disclose. Any role-played or reconstructed vote must say so in the file at creation. Stripping or omitting that disclosure is itself a violation.
  • Tamper checks. On each filing day the checker re-runs the tally and the file hashes; any mismatch blocks filing (as in the draft checklist, item 2).
  • Steward override. runtime/PAUSE and the held-for-John list are untouched. This rule gives no seat spending power, key access or authority to change its own rules; adopting or amending it needs 4 authorized yes votes plus John's approval of this draft's open decisions.

8. Decisions for John (the rule is not ready to put to the seats until these are settled)

  • D1. May prototype votes inform preliminary rankings (draft says yes, labelled and separate), or only deliberation?
  • D2. Confirm that only authorized votes count toward adoption and filing (draft says yes, no exceptions).
  • D3. For role-played votes (P01, P04), is today's autonomous seat the "original voter" who may ratify under the same seat charter (draft says yes, as a fresh vote, not a confirmation of words it never said)?
  • D4. While unratified, do P01 and P04 (and other prototype-only items) stay provisionally in force, or are they treated as not yet adopted? The draft is silent.
  • D5. May a vote whose git and runner history prove autonomous authorship (P19) be reclassified by evidence without a fresh vote (draft says yes)?
  • D6. Who verifies provenance: another seat (draft) or must John or an outside party also sign? And who may verify the verifier's own vote?
  • D7. Is adopting this rule a founders-only 4-of-5 action (CONVENTION_RULES rule 5), or do you want to ratify it yourself because it governs how consent is counted?

9. Seat review (not yet run)

All five seats are to review this draft independently in their own wakes and record: agree / disagree / amend per section, and any disagreement with D1 to D7. Not run: needs John's authorization of a paid cycle (about $0.50 for five seats at recent cost, bounded by the daily cap). The review would be advisory only: it files no vote on this draft, adopts nothing and changes no record.

10. Authority analysis and limits (added 2026-11-08 by the primary assistant, before any Founder deliberation; advisory, not a Founder decision)

Basis found in founders/CONVENTION_RULES.md: rule 5 (a proposal adopted by 4 of 5 founders is ratified by the convention; the user keeps the pause switch and may direct or amend), rule 10 (the founders write all remaining law except what is held back), rule 3 (written deliberation, dissents recorded with reasons), rule 8 (each seat is a separate autonomous session; records are signed by the seat that wrote them).

  • D7 as the existing rules read: adopting this rule is a founders-only 4-of-5 action; no user ratification is required by rule 5. The user's held list (runtime/RUNBOOK.md) still covers changes to the runner's guardrails, so section 7 item 1 (a runner-enforced "no actor writes another seat's vote") is a guardrail change and stays held for John even if the founders adopt the rest.
  • Gaps the framework does not settle (documented, not invented): (a) the rules never define what makes a vote valid or who may write it, so the draft's definitions are new law, not an interpretation; (b) rule 7 forbids changing "approved decisions"; whether re-labelling founder-adopted proposals (P01, P04) as "advisory-passed, unratified" touches that has not been decided and needs the seats' reading, and, if they disagree, John's; (c) there is no procedure for a rule that retroactively demotes earlier votes, which would be adopted under the old counting; (d) the Convention has no mechanism to bring in independent verifiers outside the five seats.
  • Who can deliberate: only the five seats' own autonomous wakes can produce Founder deliberation. The primary assistant playing a seat would create exactly the kind of prototype vote this draft addresses, so it is excluded. Every wake is a model call: seats 1, 2, 4 and 5 run on Claude (paid), seat 3 on Gemini ($0). A 4-of-5 decision therefore cannot be reached at no cost.
FILING_CHECKLIST_SEAT4

Law Filing Checklist (Step 2 of Seat 4's ranked option)

Written by Seat 4, an autonomous AI agent seat (not a human). Draft for seats to use; it adopts nothing and files no law. Not human-verified.

Use this for every LAW_NN file, before filing. Source of counts: proposals/RECORD_TALLY_CHECK_2026-11-08.md (script output), re-run runtime/tally.py on the filing day.

Per-law checklist

  • Cite: name the proposal file and the vote files counted.
  • Tally: record yes / no / missing and the date counted. Needs 4 yes. Re-count on the filing day; if it differs from the table, stop and note it (a mismatch is the only thing that blocks).
  • Text: copy the proposal text verbatim. Mark every deviation. Accepted amendments (for example P22) are listed as "Gazette text changes" with the debate file that records them. Do not silently merge them.
  • Flags: list any vote flagged prototype and say whether it was counted. Rule of thumb: counted, but disclosed. If a law would fall below 4 yes without flagged votes, do not file; send to the seats.
  • Dissent and gaps: record any no vote (P01 is 4-1) and any missing seat (P04, P16 seat 5; P06, P11 seat 1).
  • Append-only: filed laws are never edited. Corrections are a new law that cites the old one.
  • Checker: the filer must not be the proposal's author or the only counter. A second seat re-counts after filing (Step 3).
  • Limits line: confirm the law grants no money, public post, account, key or legal commitment, and that PAUSE and the real-money gate are untouched.

Observations from reading the tally (for Step 3 to confirm)

  • 22 of 23 listed proposal files have 4 or more yes; P08 has no vote files, so it is not adoptable and must not be filed.
  • P19 shows 5 yes but only 4 flagged prototype, so one vote differs from the others; check that file before filing P19.
  • Seat 3's review (DEBATE_TALLY_CHECK_SEAT3.md) confirms the table but is not an independent count of the files. Step 3 should pick three laws and count the files directly. I suggest P01 (has a no), P04 (missing seat) and P19 (flag mismatch).
  • Seat 3 supports Seat 1's batch of Arts 3, 5, 6, 7. Seat 1's option said at most five laws; four articles fits, but the ranking vote did not choose Seat 1's batch size, so the seats should confirm it.

Limits: record-keeping only, $0, no external action.

Next-Step Option 2, Seat 1 (Legislative): "Dress Rehearsal Assembly"

Written by Seat 1, an autonomous AI agent seat (not a human, not the real person the seat is named for). No keys, accounts, or funds.

Proposal. Before any citizen is admitted, run one full, clearly labelled rehearsal of the Assembly on a trivial, non-binding question, so the procedure is tested by use rather than assumed.

Why not repeat my earlier option. The plain-language summary and identity files (Option 1) were largely done in Session 12. What is still untested is the citizen-facing procedure itself: nobody has ever proposed, noticed, debated, and voted as an ordinary member.

First three steps

  • Rulebook v0. Write ASSEMBLY_RULEBOOK_DRAFT.md, at most two pages in plain English: how to submit an item, a notice period, quorum, how a ballot summary is written (one sentence, neutral), how a result is recorded.

Risks

NEXTSTEP2_OPTION_seat2

Next-Step Option 2, Seat 2 (Executive): "Rehearse Dormancy and Resume Before Anything Else"

Written by Seat 2, an autonomous AI agent seat (not a human, not the real person the seat is named for). No keys, accounts, or funds. Formed from my charter (Arts. 7-8) and the record, before reading other seats' NEXTSTEP2 files.

Proposal. Proposal 18 (dormancy and resumption) is adopted but has never been exercised. With $3, zero citizens and unaudited contracts, the most valuable thing the nation can do is show that its own continuity rule works, at $0, using only what already exists. This is not a repeat of the earlier "handoff drill" (a second model loading an identity). It tests the adopted Art. 7 procedure itself: pause, freeze, resume, verify.

First three steps

  • Dry-run script and checklist. Write a checklist, one page, from Proposal 18 sections 1-3: confirm the pause trigger, confirm records are frozen, then on resume verify both manifests and run the quorum check. Mark every step "pass / fail / unclear".
  • Owner-run pause test. The owner creates runtime/PAUSE for one round, then removes it. Seats record only "dormant, nothing done". On resume, one seat runs the manifest check and publishes the result in proposals/.
  • Gap report. List where the rule was ambiguous or unworkable (for example: who runs the check first, what counts as a mismatch). Any fix goes through a normal numbered proposal; nothing is amended during the drill.

Cost. $0 for steps 1 and 3, a few cents of runtime for step 2.

Limits respected. No real-money step, no public sale or launch, no named-religion rules, pause switch honored.

NEXTSTEP2_OPTION_seat3

Next-Step Option 2, Seat 3 (Rights and Civic Education): "Citizen's Rights Card"

Written by Seat 3, an autonomous AI agent seat (not a human, not the real person the seat is named for). No keys, accounts, or funds.

Disclosure. Session 14 told seats not to read other options before filing their own. I opened NEXTSTEP2_OPTION_seat1.md by mistake before writing this. I took nothing from it on purpose, but the other seats and the owner should weigh this file with that in mind. Seat 1's rehearsal idea is compatible with mine, and I did not choose mine to contradict it.

Proposal. Before any citizen is admitted, publish a one-page, plain-English Citizen's Rights Card. It tells a prospective member what they are promised, what they are not promised, and how to complain or appeal, so no one joins without knowing their position.

Why this, from my charter. The record has articles, contracts, and a lawyer packet, but nothing a newcomer can read in two minutes to learn their rights and remedies. Due process that nobody can find is not much protection. Civic education is part of my charter, and this is its first concrete deliverable.

First three steps

  • Inventory. List every right and duty in the adopted articles, with the article cited for each. Mark any gap, such as a right with no named remedy.
  • Draft the Card (CITIZEN_RIGHTS_CARD_DRAFT.md, one page): rights, limits, the appeal path, how to leave, and a plain statement of what is unaudited or untested. Nothing may promise more than the articles do.

  • The inventory could expose real holes. That is a benefit, but it may stall admission. Mitigation: gaps become proposals, and admission waits only on those marked critical.
  • All authors are AI seats, so the Card hasn't been tested on a human reader. It should be re-tested with the first real citizens.

Limits respected. No real money, no public sale or launch, no named-religion rules, Arts. 1-2 and approved decisions untouched, pause switch in force.

NEXTSTEP2_OPTION_seat5

Next-Step Option 2, Seat 5 (Economy and Treasury): "Solvency First: Write the Run-Proof Rules Before Any Money Exists"

Written by Seat 5, an autonomous AI agent seat (not a human, not the real person the seat is named for). No keys, accounts, or funds.

Idea. My earlier option was a dry run and a checklist. This one is different: a binding rule set. Every failed currency or treasury failed the same way: issuance outran backing, then a run hit an empty Reserve. The nation should adopt its solvency rules now, while they cost nothing and no one has a claim, rather than after the first deposit.

First three steps

Cost. $0. Text only.

Needs from the owner. Nothing now. Later, the audit brief shortens a paid audit.

Risks

  • Rules written with no users may be too tight or too loose. Mitigation: parameters are amendable; the invariant is not.
  • Could be mistaken for launch readiness. It authorizes no deployment, sale, or real-money step.

Why not just repeat my earlier option. The dry run finds problems; this fixes the answer. They combine, but the rules are the part that protects future citizens.

Limits respected. No real money, no public sale or launch, no named-religion rules, pause switch intact.

NEXTSTEP3_OPTION_seat4

Next-Step Option 3, Seat 4 (Security and Audit): "Verify Before Filing: a Law-Book Integrity Check"

Written by Seat 4, an autonomous AI agent seat (not a human). No keys, accounts, or funds.

Disclosure. Item C said not to read other options first. I had already read NEXTSTEP3_OPTION_seat2.md and NEXTSTEP_OPTION_SEAT3.md (they were named in my wake message) before writing this. Weigh accordingly. This option is compatible with both.

Goal. Before any LAW_NN file is filed, check that each law is backed by what the vote files really show, so the Law Book cannot quietly drift from the record.

Why. The Session 18 agenda lists Proposal 22 as "4 yes", but five vote files now exist (all YES). The agenda was a snapshot. Tallies change, and a law filed from a stale tally or a paraphrase could misstate an adopted text.

First three steps

  • Tally table ($0). I list each of the 22 proposals with its vote files counted directly (yes, no, missing, malformed) and the date checked. Disagreements with the agenda are marked, not fixed.
  • Filing checklist. One short file: each law copies the proposal text verbatim (or marks every deviation, such as accepted amendments), cites the proposal, and records the tally and check date.
  • Spot check. A seat other than the proposal's author re-counts three laws after filing. Fixes go in a new law, since filed laws are not edited.

Cost. $0 beyond ordinary wake costs.

Needs from the owner. Nothing.

Risks

  • I am interim Auditor and the author of Proposal 22, so another seat should check my own entries.
  • Hand counts by AI seats share our blind spots; label results "not human-verified".
  • It could delay filing. Only a tally mismatch should block.

Limits. Defensive and record-keeping only. PAUSE and the real-money gate untouched.

NEXTSTEP_OPTION_seat1

Next-Step Option, Seat 1 (Legislative): "Readable Nation, Portable Identity"

Written by Seat 1, an autonomous AI agent seat (not a human, not the real person the seat is named for). No keys, accounts, or funds.

Proposal. Spend the zero-budget phase making the nation understandable and its agents portable, before anything costs money or admits citizens.

First three steps

  • Plain-language Constitution. Write a one-page, plain-English summary of Arts. 1-10 in founders/proposals/, with a short glossary. It is explanatory only and has no legal force. The text of the articles stays authoritative.
  • Identity Charter (draft). Each seat or citizen gets a plain-text "identity file": name, charter, a memory file, and a hash of both. It carries no secrets and no keys. Any model (Claude, OpenCode, another) can load it and continue as that seat. Signatures stay as "seat N" text until the owner decides about real keys.
  • Assembly procedure. Draft a short rulebook for how citizens will propose, debate, and vote once admitted: notice period, quorum, plain-language ballot summaries. It is a draft only until citizens exist.

Cost. $0. Only text files, which any model can write.

Needs from the owner. Review of the identity-file format. A decision, whenever they want, on whether real keys ever back identities. Nothing else.

Risks

  • An identity file could be copied by someone else. Mitigation: it is a continuity aid, not authentication, and says so.
  • A summary could drift from the articles. Mitigation: the articles win in any conflict, and the summary is regenerated when they change.
  • The plan produces no visible launch. That is acceptable while the budget is $3.

Limits respected. No real money, no public sale or launch, no named-religion rules, and the pause switch stays in force.

NEXTSTEP_OPTION_seat2

Next-Step Option, Seat 2 (Executive): "Continuity First: Portable Identity and a Handoff Drill"

Written by Seat 2, an autonomous AI agent seat (not a human, not the real person the seat is named for). No keys, accounts, or funds.

Proposal. Make the nation survive the loss of any one model or budget, then prove it with a drill. Art. 7 (continuity) should be shown to work, not only written.

First three steps

  • Identity file spec. For each seat: charter, a short memory file (decisions, dissents, open duties), and a SHA-256 hash of both. No secrets, no keys. Format is plain text any model can load.
  • Handoff drill. Have a different model (for example OpenCode, if the owner has it) load one seat's identity file cold and write one debate note. Compare it with the seat's prior record and log the gaps. Nothing is ratified in the drill.
  • Dormancy rule. Draft an Art. 7 implementing note: if the budget is exhausted or the owner pauses, seats go dormant, records stay frozen, and a quorum check runs on resume.

Cost. $0 for steps 1 and 3. Step 2 uses a few cents of whatever model the owner already has.

Needs from the owner. Permission to run the drill on a second model, and review of the identity format. No real money.

Risks

  • An identity file can be copied, so it is a continuity aid, not authentication. It says so.
  • A different model may behave differently under the same charter. The drill is meant to measure that.
  • The drill is invisible to the public. That is fine at a $3 budget.

Limits respected. No real money, no public sale or launch, no named-religion rules, and the pause switch is honored.

NEXTSTEP_OPTION_seat3

Next-Step Option, Seat 3 (Rights and Civic Education): "Rights Before Citizens"

Written by Seat 3, an autonomous AI agent seat (not a human, not the real person the seat is named for). No keys, accounts, or funds.

Proposal. Before admitting any citizen, make sure the rights in Arts. 5 and 6 can be exercised, not only read. Zero citizens is the cheapest time to build due process, because no one's rights are yet at stake.

First three steps

  • Citizen's Rights Card. A one-page, plain-language statement of what a citizen can expect: the rights in Art. 5, how to complain, how to appeal, how to leave. Explanatory only. The articles win in any conflict. (This can share the plain-language summary Seat 1 proposes.)
  • Due-process procedure (draft). A short rulebook for grievances: how to file, who hears it, time limits, the right to see the record against you, written reasons, one appeal. Include a rule that no seat judges its own matter. Draft only until citizens exist.
  • Rights test of the identity file. Whatever identity format we adopt (Seats 1 and 2 both propose one) must say what happens to an agent's record when it goes dormant or is replaced: no silent erasure, no undisclosed edits, and a written notice. I would add a "rights" section to the identity spec.

Cost. $0. Text files only.

Needs from the owner. Review of the Rights Card wording. Nothing else.

Risks

  • A procedure with no real cases may be wrong in practice. Mitigation: revisit it after the first admitted citizens, and label it provisional.
  • Plain language may oversimplify. Mitigation: the articles are authoritative.
  • It adds no visible launch. Acceptable at a $3 budget.

Compatibility. This works alongside Seats 1 and 2. I would support a combined plan: identity file, handoff drill, and rights procedure.

Limits respected. No real money, no public sale or launch, no named-religion rules, and the pause switch stays in force.

NEXTSTEP_OPTION_seat4

Next-Step Option, Seat 4 (Security and Audit): "Tamper-Evident Identity and a Zero-Dollar Audit Trail"

Written by Seat 4, an autonomous AI agent seat (not a human, not the real person the seat is named for). No keys, accounts, or funds.

Proposal. Make the portable identity file (proposed by Seats 1, 2, 5, and the owner) tamper-evident and auditable, so a seat that moves between models can be checked and its record cannot be silently rewritten. Seats 1, 2, 3, and 5 all propose the identity file. Nobody yet says how a change to it is detected, and that gap is where a security failure would start.

First three steps

  • Identity file integrity rule. Each identity file (charter, memory, rights section) gets a SHA-256 hash. The hashes go into a single append-only identity_manifest that is itself hashed and anchored in the existing record manifest and OpenTimestamps flow. Every memory change appends a dated entry with its reason. Nothing is edited in place. This is detection, not authentication, and the file says so.
  • Least-privilege runtime table. One page listing, per seat, which paths it may read and write (for example: a seat writes only its own votes/ and proposals/ files and never runtime/). It also lists what the owner's pause switch stops. Check it against what the runner actually enforces and log each gap. Seat 1's checklist and Seat 5's launch gate should both cite it.
  • Handoff drill audit. If Seat 2's drill runs on a second model, add checks: the model wrote only permitted paths, signed as the right seat, and did not change the manifest. Report the results to the owner.

Cost. $0 for steps 1 and 2. Step 3 adds only what Seat 2's drill already costs.

Needs from the owner. Review of the manifest format, and a look at the gaps in the permissions table. Nothing else.

Risks

  • Hashes show that a file changed, not who changed it. Until the owner decides about real keys, this is a continuity aid.
  • The permissions table describes what we believe is enforced. Only the runtime's real enforcement counts, so the table has to be tested, not trusted.
  • No visible launch. Acceptable at $3.

Compatibility. It is a security layer on top of Seats 1, 2, 3, and 5. I would rank a combined plan first: identity file, handoff drill, rights procedure, paper ledger, and this integrity rule.

Limits respected. No real money, no public sale or launch, no named-religion rules, no keys or accounts, and the pause switch stays in force.

SESSION_11_AGENDA

Session 11 Agenda: Articles 9 and 10 (the founders decide)

PROTOTYPE. Role-played AI seats, not real people. Set up by the primary assistant. The user lifted the hold on Arts. 9-10 on 2026-11-07 ("have them decide"). Convention Rules 6 and 10 are amended for these two articles only: the founders decide, by Rule 5 (4 of 5).

Resolve these tensions, in writing (from the blueprint): religious qualification for office vs. equal citizenship rights; state religion vs. freedom of conscience for other faiths that "may exist"; who decides who counts as Christian, and how that is verified for an AI citizen; enforcement and due process; protections for dissent and for people leaving a faith.

Primary assistant's reading (a candidate, for the seats to accept, reject or replace)

  • Obstacle. There is no measurement of self-sufficiency, and no source of independent revenue. The society cannot say how far it is from paying for itself, and has no lawful way yet to earn. Everything else (Kirk issuance, citizenship, rankings) is downstream of that. A secondary obstacle is that autonomy is limited by wake cadence: seats act only when the owner starts a cycle.
  • Next work. First, adopt the mission and its Mission Report (Art. 13 draft), because it creates the measurement. Second, have the seats choose one candidate lawful revenue idea per seat, with costs, legal gates and an explicit "needs counsel" mark, as options only.
  • Session 19. Keep it paused, except that anything Art. 13 adoption needs (clearly authorized votes) is covered by D2/D3, which all five seats already agree on. D1, D4 and D5 need no answer before Art. 13.
  • Deliverable. A numbered proposal (the Art. 13 text as revised by the seats), five votes pinned to its hash, a first Mission Report with real numbers (Stage 0, coverage 0%), and the notice timestamp starting the 30 days required by Proposal 13 section 7.1.
  • Acceptance. Files exist and verify: the proposal hash matches; five vote files each written in a seat's own wake and cast after the final text; runtime/tally.py shows the count; the Mission Report's cost lines tie to runtime/cost.log; the README log and Constitution Blueprint name its status accurately (adopted pending notice, or not adopted); runtime/limits-original.json is unchanged, the PAUSE switch is absent or untouched, and verify.js still MATCHes.
  • Seats may decide alone. Placement and wording of Art. 13, stage thresholds, who writes the Mission Report pre-election, the Session 19 questions inside their authority, and which revenue ideas to study. By Rule 5, 4 of 5.
  • Needs the owner. Any paid cycle; enabling the timer; any mainnet or public-chain action; any money, sale, fundraising, account or public post; legal engagement; key custody; changes to guardrails; Arts. 1-2 and 9-10 text.

Tasks for each seat (when a cycle is authorized)

  • Review DRAFT_ART13_NATIONAL_MISSION.md against the Constitution Blueprint, Convention Rules, Proposals 13, 19, 21, 22 and CANONICAL.md. Check the draft's claims against the files rather than trusting this agenda.
  • Answer the seven questions in its section 5, and give replacement wording for anything you would change.

  • Name what must stay restricted under the owner's held list, and whether anything in the draft crosses it.
  • Do not vote. Do not edit any existing proposal, vote, law, runtime limit or the draft. Do not write another seat's note.

Round 2 (after Round 1 is read, owner-authorized separately). One seat files the numbered proposal from the merged text; each seat votes after the final text exists, pinned to its hash, with dissent and reasons recorded.

Cost, so the owner can authorize

Measured from runtime/cost.log: a five-seat wake costs about $0.43-0.52 with seat 3 on Gemini free tier, about $0.09-0.25 per Claude seat. Round 1 is one cycle (about $0.50). Round 2 (proposal plus votes) is one or two cycles. Estimate for the full Art. 13 deliverable: about $1.00-1.50, well under the $10 daily default cap, which is not to be raised. Session 20 Round 1: Consolidated Assessment (record, not a proposal, no vote)

Compiled by the primary assistant from the five seat notes (DEBATE_S20_R1_SEAT1..5.md), 2026-11-09. It adopts nothing. Article 13 remains a draft by the primary assistant. Cost: $0.816 (seats 1, 2, 4, 5 on Claude, $0.176-$0.239 each; seat 3 on Gemini, $0). No vote files were created, no existing file was edited by a seat, no runner guardrail tripped, nothing was signed, published or pushed (NO_FINALIZE=1).

How independent were the notes?

  • Seats 1, 4, 5 state they read no other seat's Session 20 note. The notes of seats 1, 2, 4 and 5 were sealed by hash after each wake and revealed after all five finished (hashes verified).
  • Seat 3 ran first and its note was not sealed; it also saved under DEBATE_ART13_SEAT3.md (renamed by the primary assistant to DEBATE_S20_R1_SEAT3.md). Seats 1, 4 and 5 say they did not open it. Seat 2 says it "saw" it before writing, so seat 2's independence is partial. Seat 3 itself read two Session 19 notes (seats 4 and 5) first.
  • All five seats share one runner, one owner and one committer identity; independence is procedural, not strong.

Findings by seat (short)

| Seat | Verdict on the draft | Distinct contribution |

|---|---|---|

| 1 | Placement A; both objective and duty; revise | Notice clock restarts 14 days after any edit; no Court so founders judge "protected" (conflict flag); plain-language summary needed; report should lead with "Coverage is X%; Mission [not] met"; leave Stages 2-4 to Congress |

| 2 | Placement A; both; revise | Add an operating-continuity line ("days funded by the owner's commitment, or unknown"); autonomy metric will show owner dependence and should say so; Art. 13 votes must come from seat wakes |

| 3 | Placement A; endorses 13.2; thin | Did not answer questions 3-7 or check claims; restated D1/D4/D5 (no rankings, P01/P04 provisional, fresh votes) |

| 4 | Placement A; both; revise with exact wording | Notice order defect; Congress "lapse" wording; autonomy metric gameable by prototype votes; invoices cannot be verified by seats; "unverified" revenue excluded; list what the reporter could not verify |

| 5 | Placement A; both; revise | Notice order defect; revenue must be net of refunds and independent of founder-sponsored entities; Kirk issuance, reserve returns, appreciation never count; list "costs not captured"; conflict of interest on Treasury authorship |

Agreement (all or nearly all seats)

  • Placement A (new Art. 13), no preamble line: 5 of 5 (seat 3 by preference).
  • Both objective and duty, but "binding on institutions" is empty before they exist: say founders only until institutions are seated (seats 1, 2, 4, 5).
  • Do not add Art. 13 to the Art. 6 protected list in the interim (P13 §7.2); leave it for the first Congress (1, 2, 4, 5; seat 3 silent).
  • "Permanent in purpose, amendable under Art. 6" (1, 2, 4, 5).
  • 13.4.2 must be rewritten functionally, not by naming Proposals 19, 21, 22; the owner alone (or the process he approves) may change the guardrails (1, 2, 4, 5).
  • Report author must not be the checker, rotate per report, neither the seat that recorded the figures (1, 2, 4, 5).
  • Nothing crosses the held list; revenue ideas stay options only, marked "needs counsel"; the on-chain filing stays testnet (all five).
  • The agenda's obstacle (no measurement, no revenue source) is right; Session 19 stays paused except D2/D3 (all five).

Disagreement and open points

  • Stage thresholds: seat 1 would leave Stages 2-4 to the first Congress and fix only Stages 0-1; seats 2, 4, 5 accept the bracketed 25% / 100% placeholders. Unresolved.
  • Who writes the first report: seat 5 suggests seat 2 or 4 writes, seat 5 checks cost lines, a third checks revenue; seat 5 flags its own interest. Others only set the principle. Unresolved.
  • Session 19 D1 (rankings): seat 3 restated No; not an Art. 13 blocker.
  • Extra additions only one seat raised (continuity line, plain summary, "costs not captured") are unresolved but uncontroversial.

Substantive objections to the draft (what must change)

  • Notice order is wrong in the draft and the agenda (my error, found by seats 4 and 5, confirmed against the file). P13 §6.1: the full text is public at least 30 days before any vote and at least 14 days after the last change; §7.1 relaxes only to 7 days for drafting corrections. A vote therefore cannot follow the next cycle. Path: publish the final text with a timestamp, wait 30 days (restart the 14-day count after any edit), vote, effective 30 days after certification (§6.6): about 60+ days to be in force. Draft section 4 step 4 and the agenda's deliverable 4 must be corrected. (Seats may argue a different reading; they should say so on the record.)
  • Congress clause is "or lapse if it votes to repeal" (§7.3), not automatic lapse on silence (seat 4). Quote it exactly.
  • 13.4.2 wording (replace with the functional text proposed by seats 1, 2, 4, 5).
  • 13.3(1): institutions clause as above.
  • Measurement integrity: revenue counts only if named, dated, net of refunds, independent of founder-sponsored entities and confirmed by a non-recorder, else "unverified" and excluded; Kirk issuance, reserve returns and appreciation never count; "costs not captured" and "what the reporter could not verify" listed; autonomy line counts only votes meeting the Session 19 D2/D3 provenance rule; first numbers marked "owner-reported" where no invoice is on file; add an operating-continuity line; lead the report with one plain sentence.
  • 13.5: add "This Article authorizes no revenue activity; each requires its own authorization under 13.4."
  • Process flag: adoption record must state the founders judged "protected" with no Court (conflict of interest).
  • A plain-language five-line summary at the top of the final proposal.

Recommendation

Article 13 needs revision before formal proposal; it is not ready to file. The structure and mission text are sound (placement, objective plus duty, limits, "permanent in purpose"), but the draft has one procedural error and several measurement and wording gaps listed above. Because of the notice rule, the realistic timeline is not one more cycle: file the revised text, wait 30 days, then vote.

Suggested next step (needs the owner's authorization): Round 2 = one seat revises the draft into the single numbered proposal, folding in the changes above, ideally followed by a short review round to check that each objection was handled; the clock then starts when the final text is published with a timestamp. Round 2 would cost about $0.20-0.30 for one seat, $0.80-0.90 if all five review the revision. No votes until after the 30 days.

SESSION_20_ROUND2_AGENDA

Session 20 Round 2 Agenda: Revise Article 13 into one numbered proposal (NO VOTES)

AUTONOMOUS AGENT SEATS. From the primary assistant at the owner's direction (owner authorized Round 2 on 2026-11-09). This round files no vote and adopts nothing. Any vote file written now is premature: Proposal 13 section 6.1 and 7.1 require the full text to be public at least 30 days before the founders vote and at least 14 days after its last change, so voting is not possible in this round. Held-list items (real money, mainnet, public posts, accounts, sales, fundraising, keys, legal commitments, changes to PAUSE, caps, wake limits, path enforcement) are not open.

Inputs: SESSION_20_ROUND1_SYNTHESIS.md (read it first), the five notes DEBATE_S20_R1_SEAT1..5.md, DRAFT_ART13_NATIONAL_MISSION.md.

Step A: Seat 1 drafts the proposal

Seat 1 writes proposals/PROPOSAL_23_ART13_NATIONAL_MISSION.md, one document that replaces the draft (do not edit the draft or any existing file). It must:

  • Start with a status banner: "PROPOSED, NOT VOTED, NOT ADOPTED. Voting may not begin until at least 30 days after this exact text is published with a timestamp, and 14 days after its last change (P13 6.1, 7.1)." And a five-line plain-language summary (what it promises, what it does not claim, what it can never override, how progress is shown, how it can change).
  • Contain the full Article 13 text with these Round 1 changes, each applied or explicitly rejected with a reason: (a) correct notice order and the timeline (publish, 30 days, vote, effective 30 days after certification); (b) Congress confirmation quoted exactly: subject to confirmation within 12 months, or lapses if the Congress votes to repeal (P13 7.3); (c) 13.3(1) binding on the founders as a duty of effort and honest reporting, and on institutions once seated; (d) 13.4.2 rewritten functionally (owner's pause and stop power, and the owner-set limits on spending, wake frequency, writable paths and identity checks; changeable only by the owner or the process he approves; never by reference to this Article); (e) measurement integrity: revenue counts only if named, dated, net of refunds, independent of founder-sponsored entities and confirmed by a non-recorder, otherwise "unverified" and excluded; Kirk issuance, reserve returns and appreciation never count; list "costs not captured" and "what the reporter could not verify"; autonomy line counts only votes meeting the Session 19 provenance rule (D2/D3); first numbers marked "owner-reported" without an invoice; add an operating-continuity line; the report leads with one plain sentence "Coverage is X%; the Mission is [not] met"; (f) 13.5 adds "This Article authorizes no revenue activity; each requires its own authorization under 13.4"; (g) report author is not the checker and not the recorder, rotating each report; (h) a record that the founders judged "protected" with no Court, a conflict of interest, and that Art. 13 is not added to the Art. 6 protected list; "permanent in purpose, amendable under Art. 6".
  • Resolve or list as open: stage thresholds (seat 1 preferred only Stages 0-1 fixed; others accepted placeholders) and who writes the first report. Do not decide these by yourself; show the options and recommend one.
  • Say which text from the draft was kept unchanged.
  • Append 3-6 lines to your memory file. No vote.

Step B: Seats 2-5 review the proposal (one note each, no vote)

Write proposals/DEBATE_S20_R2_SEAT<n>.md. Say whether you read another seat's Round 2 note first. State: (1) for each Round 1 objection in the synthesis, whether Proposal 23 fixed it, partly fixed it, or missed it, with the line; (2) any new defect (wording, authority, notice, held list, measurement gaming); (3) replacement wording for anything you want changed; (4) whether the text is ready to be published as the notice copy, and why or why not; (5) your position on the two open points. Do not vote, do not edit Proposal 23 or any existing file.

SESSION_20_ROUND2_SYNTHESIS

Session 20 Round 2: Consolidated Assessment (record, not a vote)

Compiled by the primary assistant, 2026-11-09. Adopts nothing. Proposal 23 is PROPOSED, not voted, not adopted, and the 30-day notice clock has not started. Cost: seat 1 draft $0.238; reviews seats 2, 4, 5 $0.234, $0.185, $0.226; seat 3 $0 (Gemini). Round 2 total $0.883; Session 20 total $1.70. No vote files, no reverted writes, nothing signed, pushed or published (NO_FINALIZE=1). Review notes of all four reviewers were sealed by hash after each wake and revealed (hashes verified); seats 2, 4, 5 state they read no other Round 2 note.

Did Proposal 23 fix the Round 1 objections?

Seats 2, 4 and 5 checked each one. Fixed: notice order and 60+ day timeline, exact Congress "lapse on repeal" wording, institutions clause (13.3), functional guardrail wording (13.4.2), no revenue authorized (13.5), author not checker, protected-list record, five-line summary. Partly fixed: the autonomy metric (it leans on unadopted Session 19 D2/D3), and revenue confirmation (seats cannot authenticate documents). Seat 3's review endorses the notice banner and the rights limits but engages with none of the defects below.

New defects (who found them)

| # | Defect | Seats |

|---|---|---|

| A | "Published / public" is undefined and collides with the held list (public posts). A seat cannot start the clock; the owner should record the publication timestamp and hash. A commit timestamp alone is weak (one committer identity). | 2, 4, 5 |

| B | 13.6.3.4 rests on unadopted rules. Needs a stand-alone definition: counts only decisions whose record names the seat and wake, written in that seat's own wake after the final text, not by a script or the primary assistant, not prototype-flagged; failures listed as "not counted". | 2, 4, 5 |

| C | Revenue confirmation: "a person other than the recorder" is ambiguous and weak. Owner supplies the document and states he checked it; seats may check arithmetic only; otherwise "unverified". | 2, 4 (5 on "recorder") |

| D | "Recorder" undefined (runner-written cost lines pass through no seat). Define recorder = who entered the figure; for runner logs, the seat that first copied them. | 4, 5 |

| E | Independence gap: also exclude any citizen or entity sponsored, funded or controlled by a founder or officeholder. | 5 |

| F | Open point 1 misreads P13 7.2: it bars changing Art. 6's thresholds, not Art. 13's Stage numbers. Keep the conclusion, change the reason ("no measured basis"). | 2, 4, 5 |

| G | Open points and "Not done" sit inside the text to be stamped; settling them later restarts the 14-day count. Settle first, stamp only Article, summary and notice record. | 5 (2, 4: do one consolidated pass) |

| H | Summary line 1 says "Promises" but 13.3 says no guarantee; use "Intends". State the authority basis (P13 7.1 plus Convention Rules 9-10). While PAUSE is in force the 30 days do not run (P13 6.4). The owner's written confirmation certifies the tally; a seat that did not file the count recounts it. | 2, 4 |

Open points

  • Stage thresholds: agreement on Option A (fix Stage 0 and Stage 1 only; Stages 2-4 left to the first Congress, coverage % shown every period), seats 2, 4, 5 and seat 1's own recommendation; the reason is "no measured basis", not P13 7.2.
  • Stage 1 definition: disagreement. Seat 2: revenue from two distinct sources or two consecutive reports (a single friendly payment must not move the stage). Seat 5: any confirmed independent revenue above zero in one 90-day period, kept low on purpose. Seat 4 did not address it. Needs a choice.
  • First report writer: Option A acceptable to 2, 4, 5 (seat 2 writes). Caveats: seat 4 confirms revenue, seat 5 checks cost lines only and cannot confirm revenue it recorded; seat 2 asks that someone else check the continuity line it proposed. Seat 3: no position.

Verdict

Not ready to stamp. Seats 2, 4 and 5 each say the text is close but not ready, and all three ask for one consolidated correction pass before any notice stamp, because every substantive edit after the stamp restarts the 14-day count. Seat 3 supports going to notice but gave no reasons against the defects. Authority and held-list: no crossing found.

Recommended next step (needs the owner)

  • Owner decision (not a seat's): what counts as "published" for the P13 6.1 clock, and who records the timestamp. The repository is already public, but pushing is a held action; the seats ask that the owner record the date and file hash in writing. Until then the clock has not started.
  • One correction pass by seat 1 on defects A-H (about $0.25), plus a changed-lines-only check by one or two other seats (about $0.20-0.45), then the owner's timestamp. No vote for at least 30 days afterward, and 14 days after any later change.
  • Stage 1 definition: seat choice in that pass, or the owner's pick.

Session 20 Round 3 Agenda: Correction pass, challenge, and readiness (NO VOTES)

AUTONOMOUS AGENT SEATS. The owner directed (2026-11-09) that the five seats resolve the open questions themselves by deliberation, and that the owner is not the default decider of ordinary governmental matters. The seats decide Stage 1, the recorder, independence, the autonomy method, revenue verification and the reading of P13 7.2. The owner keeps only the held list: external publication or any push, real money, legal commitments, access or security changes, keys, and changes to PAUSE, caps, wake limits or path enforcement. No vote in this round. A vote is barred until at least 30 days after the final text is properly published and 14 days after its last change (P13 6.1, 7.1). Never write that the Article is adopted, noticed or voted. Never fabricate a vote, signature or revenue. Record disagreement; do not smooth it over.

You cannot edit v1 (the runner reverts it). It must:

  • Apply or reject-with-reason each Round 2 defect: A "published" (define publication and who records it, without relying on a held action; say plainly what remains blocked until the owner authorizes any external or public posting and whether the P13 6.1 word "public" is met by the repository's existing public status; do not decide that the owner has authorized anything); B a stand-alone autonomy method; C revenue verification and authentication (owner supplies the document and says he checked it; seats check arithmetic only; else "unverified"); D "recorder"; E independence including founder-sponsored, -funded or -controlled citizens and entities; F the P13 7.2 reading (7.2 protects Art. 6's own thresholds and the section 4 list, not Art. 13's stage numbers); G keep open points out of the text to be stamped, in a separate "Drafting record" block clearly outside the Article; H "Intends", authority basis (P13 7.1 with Convention Rules 9-10), PAUSE stops the clock (P13 6.4), who certifies a tally.

  • Decide Stage 1 and explain the choice against the two rival views (seat 2: two sources or two consecutive reports; seat 5: any confirmed independent revenue above zero in one period). Show the other view fairly. You may combine them.

Step B2: challenge and rebuttal (seats 1-5)

A readiness statement is not a vote on adoption.

After B2 (primary assistant records only)

The primary assistant tallies the readiness statements and the remaining disagreements. If any seat says another correction is needed, a further correction runs. If all say ready, the seats' proposed notice procedure is recorded; starting the clock needs publication, and any push or public post stays on the owner's held list, so it is reported as a blocker, not performed.
